The #852 invariant — every example program is either run or excluded with a reason — depended on directory depth, which is not evidence about whether CI should run something. `Discover` now walks `examples/` to any depth and reports every directory with a `main.go`, named by its slash-separated path, skipping `internal/`, `testdata/`, `node_modules/` and dot-directories; `deploy/ax/entrypoint` is recorded as Skip with its reason (it exits 2 without an agent command and waits on AX's egress proxy in a real boot; its unit tests cover those paths). Deliberately not changed: the `main.go` rule itself — recipe directories with no Go stay outside this gate and under `recipecheck`. The `AGENTS.md` rule, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `dev/README.md` now state the any-depth form instead of `examples/*/main.go`. ([#1417](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1417))
- **In an AX task, a mistyped `CORE_AGENT_GOOGLE_AUTH` failed about credentials, a wrong `--wait-for-egress` host spun until AX killed the pod, and the prompt landed in every runner log.** Three operator surprises in the #1395 image. `Injected`, `true` or any other non-`injected` value fell through to the ADC chain and died inside the sandbox with "could not find default credentials", pointing at the wrong thing; `pkg/models.Resolve` and `pkg/mcp`'s `auth.google_oauth` path now refuse it by name (`CORE_AGENT_GOOGLE_AUTH="Injected": want "injected" or unset`) before any provider is built. `ax-agent-entry`'s egress wait had no deadline and installed no signal handler until after it; it now runs under a `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` context and takes `--egress-timeout` (default `0`, unbounded — the golden boot's length is Substrate's to decide), exiting 1 on either without running the turn. The "started agent" line logged the whole argv, `-p "<prompt>"` included; it now logs the binary and argument count, with the argv at debug. Deliberately not changed: `0` stays the timeout default, because a bounded golden boot would exit the template command non-zero at a moment nobody chose; the final image's `debian:bookworm-slim` base stays tag-pinned (recorded on the issue as a conscious choice). ([#1419](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1419))
- **The slim-embed gate could not see weight arriving through a type-only import.** `verify-slim-deps` (#1397) denylists the named heavy subsystems, but `pkg/agent`'s imports of `pkg/eventlog` and `pkg/attach` for their types already carry `gorm.io/gorm` and `github.com/gorilla/websocket` into a "slim" graph of 546 packages, and nothing would notice the next such edge. The script now also enforces a transitive package-count ceiling (`SLIM_MAX_PACKAGES`, 570) and names the known heavy edges in its header, so a PR that adds weight has to raise the number and say what came in. Deliberately not changed: the edges themselves — splitting the types out of `pkg/eventlog`/`pkg/attach` is the real fix and is decided under the promised-subset work. ([#1415](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1415))
- **`autonomous.Resume` now aborts on `SessionLock.Lost()` (`eventlog.ErrLeaseLost`) without writing split-brain checkpoints, and `SessionLock` signals lease loss when database errors exceed the stale window.**
  - **What was broken:** `SessionLock.Lost()` (`pkg/eventlog/lock.go`) documented that callers should select on `lock.Lost()` alongside their work context and abort when it closes, but nothing outside `lock_test.go` called `Lost()`. If a resumed worker stalled past the 30-second lease window and a second worker stole the lock, the first worker kept executing turns and emitted per-turn and detached final checkpoints into the successor's session. In addition, `SessionLock.beat()` treated every `res.Error != nil` as transient forever, so a database outage lasting longer than `staleAfter` never closed `Lost()`, and `docs/site/src/content/docs/embed/api.md` falsely claimed that `autonomous.Run` also acquired `AcquireLock`.
  - **What landed:** `autonomous.Resume` derives a lease-bound context that cancels with `eventlog.ErrLeaseLost` when `lock.Lost()` closes, selects on `lock.Lost()` during deferred `NextWakeAt` waits, checks `leaseLost(lock)` before and after turns, and suppresses both per-turn and detached final checkpoints once the lease is lost. `SessionLock.beat()` tracks `lastHeartbeat` and signals lease loss when a database error occurs after the **loss window** has elapsed — `staleAfter` minus one heartbeat interval (25s at the defaults; tunable via `eventlog.WithLockHeartbeat`). The window is one interval shorter than the point at which `AcquireLock` lets a newcomer steal the row, because a fast-failing database is noticed only on the next tick; with the two numbers equal a holder could believe it owned the lease for up to one interval after a successor had taken it. The published docs (`concepts/sessions.md`, `embed/api.md`, `run/autonomous/operations.md`) now accurately state that only `autonomous.Resume` acquires the lock, document the supported **single-replica with safe restart** HA posture, and note the NTP-grade clock assumption behind the margin.
  - **Decisions and rejected alternatives:** the margin is taken from the holder's side (declare loss early) rather than the stealer's (steal later), because the latter lengthens every crash failover for the common case to protect the rare one. A lost lease is not always a stolen lease: in a single-replica deployment a database outage longer than the window has nobody to hand the lease to, so `Resume` re-proves ownership with one conditional UPDATE (`SessionLock.Reaffirm`) and writes its final checkpoint when the row is still its own — the partial turn's accounting is kept, and the run still ends with `ErrLeaseLost` so the process restarts. `Release` is bounded by the heartbeat timeout and skipped outright after a steal, because the previous unbounded `DELETE` would have hung the exit against the very database that stalled. ([#1361](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1361))

#### Other (Cleanup)
- **Harden durable write gate (`pkg/approval`), recorded-effect outbox (`internal/effects`), `pkg/permissions`, `pkg/eventlog`, `pkg/attach`, and MCP `readOnlyHint` trust (`pkg/mcp`).**
  - **What went wrong:** (1) `internal/effects.outbox.beforeRun` always passed `exemptTrailingConfirmation = true` to `scanHistory`, so a crash mid-execution after confirmation replay (`ictx.UserContent() == nil` on auto-resume) silently skipped the dangling mutating call; moreover, an `ambiguous_prior_effect` refusal or reused `FunctionCall.ID` self-cleared dangling confirmation-gated or mutating calls in `scanAccumulator` and recorded refusals in `completions`. (2) `internal/effects` and `pkg/permissions` omitted `call_peer` (`ClassMutating`) and `sciontool_status` (`ClassReadOnly`) from their built-in tool class tables, `isSyntheticRepairEvent` ignored `ev.CustomMetadata["kind"] == "tool_tail_repair"`, and `SubSessionScannerFor` only inspected `<parent>:sub:<toolName>:<callID>` (missing `<parent>:sub:<toolName>` and `<parent>:sub:bg.<bgID>`). (3) `approval.writeGate.beforeTool` only called `CheckMutatingToolCall(toolName, key)` (missing `args`), bypassing bash denylist, credential/control-plane write guards, path-scope deny rules, and `mcp:<tool>*` deny rules on `on_mutation=apply` and before parking on `require_approval`, while headless `require_approval` / `on_mutation=apply` runs failed with `ErrNoPrompter` or double-prompted inside `permissions.Gate` because pre-authorized mutations were not propagated through context. (4) `approval.prepareEdit` mutated a nil `args` map into an empty map after persisting the approval decision, causing `verdict: "edit"` on nil `args` to execute with unedited args. (5) `eventlog.agentApprovalDecisionRow` lacked `size:191` on `VARCHAR` composite-index columns (`SessionID`, `InvocationID`, `FunctionCallID`) for MySQL `utf8mb4`, `Handle.AckEffects` appended a user-role text event into LLM conversation history, and `enrichLongRunningPark` left `ParkedCall.CallID` empty and read `Request["subject"]` instead of `Request["key"]`. (6) `pkg/attach` drain-gated `GET /parks`, lacked `GET /parks/{app}/{sid}`, omitted evicted/persisted sessions in `doListParks`, omitted `CallID`/`Key`/`Policy`/`Stale`/`VerdictFormat`/`ChangeSet` on `ProposedCall`, rejected chunked or mid-turn `ack-effects` requests without a `409 Conflict` active-turn check, and fell back from `verifiedApprover` to `"anon"`. (7) `mcp.ServerSpec` had no `trust_read_only_hint` toggle to strip untrusted MCP server `readOnlyHint` annotations (`mast #573`).
  - **What was fixed:** `internal/effects` passes `hasConfirmationResponse(ictx.UserContent())` to `scanHistoryInternal`, preserves `priorUnpaired` calls across `isAmbiguousRefusalResponse` responses, recognizes `kind == "tool_tail_repair"`, classifies `call_peer` as mutating and `sciontool_status` as read-only, and scans `<parent>:sub:<toolName>` and `<parent>:sub:bg.<bgID>` child sessions. `pkg/permissions` adds `(*Gate).CheckMutatingToolCallWithArgs`, `(*Gate).AuthorizeMutationCall`, and `(*Gate).RevokeMutationCall`, wired into `pkg/approval` (`beforeTool`, `afterTool`, `prepareEdit`, and `RecordMutationVerdict`) alongside nil-`args` edit refusal (`edit_refused`). `pkg/eventlog` adds `size:191` to `agentApprovalDecisionRow`, emits a state-only metadata event in `AckEffects`, and adds `ParkedCall.CallID` populated with `Request["key"]`. `pkg/attach` serves `GET /parks` and `GET /parks/{app}/{sid}` without drain-gating, merges persisted sessions into `doListParks`, populates full `ProposedCall` metadata, enforces `409 Conflict` when a turn is active in `executeAckEffects`, and requires a verified approver identity. `pkg/mcp` adds `ServerSpec.TrustReadOnlyHint` and `(ServerSpec).TrustReadOnlyHints` to strip `readOnlyHint` annotations when `trust_read_only_hint` is `false`. ([#1447](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1447), [#1444](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1444))
- **Harden `verify-apidiff`, `cut-ga-tag.sh` major-bump enforcement, `TestDeprecationMarkersNameTheirRemoval`, and API surface sync checks.**
  - **What went wrong:** `dev/release/cut-ga-tag.sh` allowed cutting a minor or patch GA tag while `dev/api-breaks.txt` or folded `CHANGELOG.md` sections carried acknowledged breaking changes; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ignored `CHANGELOG.md`-only PRs and `verify-apidiff` only scanned `## [Unreleased]` rather than pre-release sections since `BASE_TAG`; `TestDeprecationMarkersNameTheirRemoval` did not assert a non-zero paragraph count or scan trailing field/value comments; `docs/release-process.md` and `docs/compatibility-policy.md` did not document how `/vN` module-path renames interact with `apidiff`; and nothing verified that `docs/compatibility-policy.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`'s stability promise stayed in sync with `dev/api-surface.txt`.
  - **What was fixed:** `cut-ga-tag.sh` refuses non-major GA tags when `dev/api-breaks.txt` is non-empty or folded sections contain breaking changes; `verify-apidiff` scans all sections between `## [Unreleased]` and `BASE_TAG` and runs in `ci-docs.yml` when `CHANGELOG.md` changes; `TestDeprecationMarkersNameTheirRemoval` scans trailing comments and fails on a zero-paragraph walk; `TestEveryImportablePackageIsADecision` checks that `docs/compatibility-policy.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` list every package in `dev/api-surface.txt`; and `docs/release-process.md` + `docs/compatibility-policy.md` document the `/vN` rename procedure and `extras/` scope. ([#1427](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1427))
- **Eval harness composition (`internal/evals/evalcompose`), differentiator and judge SQLite session cleanup, `outcome.PinnedLookout` alignment, and CI / nightly / release eval wiring (`dev/ci/presubmits/evals`, `.github/workflows/evals-nightly.yml`).**
  - **What was broken:** Follow-up to `#1403`: (1) `evalcompose.Build` ignored `spec.Tools.Builtin` and `filterToolsets` matched MCP tools against raw un-namespaced tool names whereas `pkg/mcp.Build` (`withNamespace`) names toolsets `<server>_mcp_tool_set` and prefixes tools as `<server>_<tool>` (`sanitizePrefix(name) + "_" + t.Name()`), silently dropping every tool listed under `spec.Tools.MCP[].Tools`; (2) `evalEchoModel.GenerateContent` only inspected `req.Tools["finish_task"]`, which ADK providers leave unset (`req.Config.Tools` holds the declarations), and `evalcompose/pricing.go` imported `github.com/go-steer/core-models/pricing` directly instead of `pkg/pricing`; (3) `outcome.PinnedLookout` was `"v0.23.0"` while the repository pins `v0.36.0` and was omitted from `imagepin.Lookout.Literals`; (4) `differentiators`, `judge`, and `outcome` never closed the SQLite `session.Service` returned by `database.NewSessionService` (and `cmd/outcome` leaked its `/tmp/mast-outcome-<pid>` scratch directory on normal exit); (5) `cmd/evals` unconditionally walked upward from `os.Getwd()` for `.agents/config.json` even on `--tier=deterministic` with no `-c` flag; and (6) `dev/tools/evals`, `dev/ci/presubmits/evals`, `.github/workflows/evals-nightly.yml`, and the pre-release outcome gate in `docs/release-process.md` were not yet wired.
  - **What landed:** `evalcompose.Build` now filters `opts.Tools` against `spec.Tools.Builtin` (`filterTools`) and delegates per-tool MCP filtering to `mcp.FilterToolset(ts, al.Server, al.Tools)` (matching both `<server>` and `<server>_mcp_tool_set` toolset names). `evalEchoModel` checks both `req.Tools` and `req.Config.Tools` (`hasToolDeclared`), `evalcompose` imports `pkg/pricing` and adds `CloseSessionService` + `DiscoverProvidersFrom`, `differentiators`, `judge`, and `outcome` close their SQLite session services and scratch directories on exit, `outcome.PinnedLookout` is updated to `"v0.36.0"` and tracked in `imagepin.Lookout.Literals`, `cmd/evals` adds `-c` and skips ambient `.agents` discovery on `--tier=deterministic`, and `dev/tools/evals`, `dev/ci/presubmits/evals`, `dev/tools/ci`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/evals-nightly.yml`, and `docs/release-process.md` wire all three eval tiers. ([#1458](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1458))
- **Wire per-agent pricing catalog, subagent MCP/skill/root scoping, prompt files, compaction, and guardrails in `agent.RunOneShot` and `compose.Run`.**
  - **What broke:** `agent.RunOneShot` (`#1404`) did not build a pricing catalog from `Config.Model.Pricing`, `.agents/pricing.json`, or `Config.Providers` profile rates (`usage.PriceOn` used the builtin-only catalog for preflight cost ceilings and subagent turns, while `usage.PriceFor` ignored `cfg` once process-global `usage.SetCatalog` had been called), ignored `Subagents[].MCPTools`, `Subagents[].Skills` subset scoping, and `Subagents[].Root` bundles, failed to reuse the parent provider or caller-supplied `OneShotConfig.Model` when a subagent declared `tier` under a profile or auto-detected provider, allowed `output_schema` paths to fall back to `projectRoot` outside `.agents/`, and omitted `agent.system_prompt_file`, `@path` expansion on `agent.append_system_prompt`, `compaction`, `safety.watchdog`, and `checkpoint.mode`.
  - **Fix:** `usage.NewCatalogFromConfig` and `usage.PriceOnCatalog` plus `agent.WithPricingCatalog` wire a per-agent `*pricing.Catalog` through root turns, preflight cost-ceiling checks, and subagent invocations without mutating process-global catalog state. `compose.Run` pre-resolves per-subagent MCP servers, `mcp_tools` allowlists (`mcp.FilterToolset`), scoped skill bundles (`skills.Scoped`), and rooted subagent directories (`<root>/mcp.json`, `<root>/skills/`, `<root>/AGENTS.md`) via `OneShotConfig.SubagentToolsets`, while `agent.RunOneShot` enforces `mcp_tools` filtering and `.agents/`-confined `output_schema` resolution and wires `system_prompt_file`, `@path` `append_system_prompt`, compaction, watchdog, and checkpoint options. ([#1453](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1453))

#### Documentation
- **Re-gradable milestone drill and soak evidence contract (`dev/uat/gke-drill/`, `docs/release-process.md`).** Scorecards require frozen grader commit, artifact archive, two seeds/days, and second-maintainer countersignature. ([#1382](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1382))
- **Tighten `review-gate` heading and `fix:` pre-fix verification checks (`.github/workflows/review-gate.yml`).** Requires `## Adversarial review` with >= 80 non-blank chars and pre-fix failure evidence on `fix:` PRs. ([#1385](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1385))
- **Enforce 300-char `[Unreleased]` bullet cap and archive pre-3.0 `CHANGELOG.md` (`dev/tools/verify-release-notes`).** Moves `v0.1.0`–`v2.9.0` to `docs/changelog-archive/pre-v3.0.md`. ([#1387](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1387))
- **Cover all non-`e2e-*` presubmits in `dev/tools/ci` (`dev/tools/verify-version-fallback`).** Adds `verify-version-fallback` to `dev/tools/ci` and enforces coverage in `TestEveryPresubmitHasACIStep`. ([#1394](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1394))
- **Document `sap-ai-core`, `oauth2_client_credentials`, custom `headers`, and `pricing: "isolated"` on provider profiles.**
  - **What changed:** The Providers concept page, Configuration reference, `--provider` CLI flag help, and `pkg/config/providers.go` now list all eight `core-models` built-in provider profiles (`vertex-maas`, `openai`, `xai`, `sap-ai-core`, `vllm`, `sglang`, `ollama`, `openai-compatible`) and document `auth.kind: "oauth2_client_credentials"`, per-profile `headers`, and `pricing: "isolated"`. ([#1461](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1461))
- **What "slim embed" carries is now a recorded decision, and the earlier #1415 entry above misattributed one edge.** That entry says `pkg/agent`'s type-only imports carry `github.com/gorilla/websocket` through `pkg/attach`; they do not — `pkg/attach` never imports it, the edge is `google.golang.org/genai`'s Live API, pulled in by ADK regardless of anything in this repository. Measuring the real graph settled the issue: the slice (`pkg/agent` + `models` + `permissions` + `tools` + `usage`, 546 packages) carries three heavier edges on purpose — `gorm.io/gorm` core (driverless) because `eventlog.Handle` is part of `pkg/agent`'s contract; genai's websocket, not ours to remove; and `gojq`, because `json_query` and `wait_and_verify`'s predicates are jq. The only accidental edge, `pkg/agent` → the `pkg/attach` server for ~70 wire types, was extracted on `refactor/attach-wire-leaf` and measured at 546 → 543 packages with ten `apidiff` false positives (aliases resolve to their target package), so it is parked for the A2A server work rather than merged. `docs/mast-fold-design.md` decision 9, the `verify-slim-deps` header and `examples/embed/slim/README.md` now say exactly this. ([#1415](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1415))
- **The published site said nothing about `CORE_AGENT_GOOGLE_AUTH=injected` or the AX deployment target, so an operator reading the Vertex, Anthropic-on-Vertex, profile or `auth.google_oauth` pages still saw "ADC required" with no way out inside a credential-injecting sandbox.** The feature landed with a CHANGELOG bullet and an `examples/deploy/ax/README.md` but no change under `docs/site/src/content/docs/`, which AGENTS.md's "the published site moves with the code" rule requires in the same PR. `concepts/providers.md` gains an "Injected auth" section under Vertex (what it switches, that only the exact value `injected` counts, that outside a gateway it is a 401 rather than a bypass) with pointers from the Anthropic-on-Vertex and profile notes; `concepts/mcp.md` § `auth.google_oauth` says what the flag skips and that `scopes` is still validated but not sent; `examples/index.md` lists `deploy/ax` under "Serving & deployment". Deliberately not changed: `reference/configuration.md`, because the variable is read from the environment and has no `config.json` field. ([#1416](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1416))
- **`AGENTS.md` had doubled to 4,251 words and stated things that were no longer true, and every agent working in this repo loads it into its system prompt.**
  - **What was wrong:**
    - It told agents that four status checks were required; branch protection requires eight.
    - Its presubmit "full sweep" named 12 scripts and skipped four that CI runs on every PR: `verify-docs-lint`, `verify-version-fallback`, `verify-gke-drill` and `verify-release-notes`.
    - It required an `acceptance-mN.md` plan that nobody had written since May.
    - It cited `convert.go` and a Vertex credentials workaround that moved to core-models with #1296.
    - Its layout omitted `compose`, `approver`, `childenv`, `transcript` and `attachadapter`.
  - **The rewrite** is 2,224 words. AGENTS.md now holds the rules and a one-line pointer for each. The mechanics, incident history and worked examples move to the new `docs/contributor-pitfalls.md`, and the procedures already in `.agents/skills/` are linked rather than restated. A review of old against new found two sub-rules the first draft had dropped; both are restored: "`//nolint` is not the fix" and "when a variant only changes what is typical, delete the example".
  - **Lists that used to drift are no longer hand-written:**
    - Required checks come from a `gh api` command against branch protection.
    - Presubmits are "everything in `dev/ci/presubmits/` except `e2e-*`, globbed".
    - The hand-kept list of packages with a tool-description sweep is gone. `TestEveryToolRegisteringPackageHasASweep` enforces that a sweep *exists*; whether it covers conditionally registered tools is still the author's job, and the text says so.
  - **Rules new to AGENTS.md:**
    - Design docs carry a status line that is updated when the work ships or is abandoned.
    - Don't grow `run()`: reusable substrate goes in `pkg/compose`, and binary-only glue goes in a named helper in `cmd/core-agent`.
    - A per-session consumer needs a per-session getter, not the global `agentRef` (#1362).
    - Provider adapter fixes, not just new providers, go to core-models.
    - Before an `--admin` merge of a Go PR, confirm that the real `CI` run passed, because `ci-docs.yml`'s no-op jobs reuse the required check names (#1271). The `stacked-pr-order` skill gains the same note.
    - After a GA, bump the version fallback. It was missed after v3.0.0 (#1351).
  - **Stale text fixed elsewhere:** `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `dev/README.md` said four required checks; `CONTRIBUTING.md` also required `acceptance-mN.md` plans and gave a test path without `pkg/`.
  - **Kept verbatim,** because other files cite them: the section names "Adversarial review gate before every PR" (`.agents/reviewer/AGENTS.md`), "Which doc changes are user-visible" (the `changelog-bullet` skill, the self-dev rig) and "How we develop" (`dev/claude/README.md`, `review-gate.yml`).
  - **Deliberately not changed:** CHANGELOG bullet length. The skill still asks for long bullets, and capping them is #1387's decision. ([#1384](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1384))

#### Security
- **Harden `read_only` and `dev_tools` bash bundles (`pkg/permissions`).** Removes `env`/`printenv`, narrows `git` patterns to read-only forms, and adds `sort`/`uniq`/`tree`/`rg`/`git`/`go` verb profiles. ([#1352](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1352))
- **Enforce verb profiles and env-prefix allowlist on bash verb grants (`pkg/permissions`).** Applies verb profiles to session verb grants, restricts `KEY=VAL` prefixes, and warns on shell-equivalent verbs. ([#1354](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1354))
- **Widen `controlPlaneRules` and make `~/.core-agent` read-only (`pkg/permissions`).** Protects `.git/**`, every `mcp.json`, `.agents/` boot files, instruction files, and `~/.core-agent/**`. ([#1356](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1356))
- **Require daemon admin for `yolo` and `bash` in multi-session mode (`pkg/attach`, `pkg/config`).** Blocks non-admin `yolo` switches and `bash` approvals unless explicitly opted in. ([#1355](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1355))
- **Guard loopback HTTP attach listeners against DNS rebinding (`pkg/attach`, `pkg/config`).** Wraps loopback HTTP muxes in `loopbackHostGuard` and validates `Host`/`Origin` against `attach.allowed_hosts`. ([#1358](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1358))
- **The A2A client (#1396) would send the configured Bearer token to whatever endpoint the agent card named, and would follow HTTP redirects with the token attached.** `A2AClient.endpointURL` took `card.url` / `additionalInterfaces[].url` as the JSON-RPC endpoint with no scheme or host check — `validateA2AURL` ran only on config URLs — so a card saying `"url": "http://other-host/rpc"` got the token in plaintext to another host. Separately, the default `http.Client{}` followed redirects on the card GET and every JSON-RPC POST; Go strips `Authorization` only when a redirect leaves the host and keeps it for subdomains, so a same-host `https`→`http` or subdomain `302` carried the token to a URL the operator never configured, and a same-host `301`/`302` also turned the POST into a GET. Now the card URL is the trust anchor: an endpoint from the card must parse as `http(s)` with a host, must not downgrade `https` to `http`, and must share the card's host (port included) unless the new `subagents[].remote.allow_card_host_change` opts in — refused with the new `background.ErrRemoteEndpointUntrusted` otherwise — and the default client sets `CheckRedirect: http.ErrUseLastResponse`, so a redirect surfaces as `returned HTTP 3xx` from the callers that already reject every non-200. Deliberately not changed: `background.WithA2AHTTPClient` still accepts any client, so an embedder who wants redirects can have them and owns the consequence; the configured `endpoint` field is still used as given, since the operator typed it. `configuration.md` documents the rule and corrects the `timeout_seconds` default from 60 to the actual 120. ([#1435](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1435))

_Pre-release history: cut incrementally as `v3.1.0-rc.2` (2026-11-05), `v3.1.0-rc.1` (2026-11-04), `v3.1.0-dev.3` (2026-11-02), `v3.1.0-dev.2` (2026-11-02), `v3.1.0-dev.1` (2026-11-01). The GitHub Release pages for each pre-release tag retain their historical release notes; this CHANGELOG folds them into the GA entry per Keep a Changelog convention._
## [3.0.0] — 2026-10-21

core-agent 3.0 is the "proven autonomy" release: every capability it claims for running unattended now has a drill or a checked-in grader that passed on the 3.0 build. That covers an eight-hour soak, degraded modes surfaced in the transcript, a human approval gate, gated cluster changes, and an agent that took a real core-agent issue to a merged PR with no person in the loop. New since 2.9: permission mode `auto` (experimental), where an approver model judges routine calls and can refuse with a reason when nobody is watching; per-turn and per-session cost ceilings that refuse before spending; and provider profiles for OpenAI, xAI, vLLM, SGLang, Ollama and Vertex MaaS through core-models. Upgrading is a major-version move: the module path is now `github.com/go-steer/core-agent/v3`, the library builds on ADK v2, and a daemon whose agent has `bash` refuses an unauthenticated attach listener. See Breaking Changes for the rest.

### Breaking Changes

- **`pkg/pricing` is now core-models' shared price catalog, re-exported.** Its types are aliases of `github.com/go-steer/core-models/pricing` (v0.13.1), so a `pricing.Rates` or `pricing.Catalog` from core-agent *is* the core-models value. Every exported name keeps compiling; two things change. `Rates` and `ModelRates` gain a `LongContext` field, so an unkeyed composite literal of either no longer compiles (keyed literals are unaffected). `Rates.CostUSD*` bill a prompt over the model's long-context threshold at the long-context rate, which is higher; see the fix below. The generator `dev/regen-builtin-pricing` and the weekly `pricing-regen` workflow are gone: the table is regenerated in core-models for both core-agent and mast, and reaches core-agent by a version bump. The release guards now check the pinned table with `dev/tools/verify-pricing-fresh`, which runs core-models' generator in `--check` mode. ([#1328](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1328))
- **A profile's declared rate no longer overrides a model the catalog already prices.** core-models' rule is that a model any catalog prices is never billed at an operator's estimate. Declared `rates` are now the lowest layer, a fallback for models nobody publishes a price for. So a profile that extends `openai` and declares `gpt-5` at its own rate now bills `gpt-5` at the catalog's `openai/gpt-5` list price, and per-turn and per-session cost ceilings trip on that figure. To pin a rate for a catalog model, use a `pricing.json` override, which still outranks the built-in table; it may be keyed `<backend>/<model>`. Found by an old-versus-new price comparison of #1328 across 2,457 model × backend × usage-shape rows, in which this and the documented long-context and new-model rows were the only differences. ([#1328](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1328))
- **The module path is now `github.com/go-steer/core-agent/v3`.** Go's semantic import versioning makes the `/vN` suffix what decides whether a `vN` tag can be installed at all, so it moves before the first 3.0 tag. Without it, `v3.0.0-dev.1` would join v2.0.0–v2.7.0-dev.4 as a tag `go install` can never resolve (#327). To upgrade, rewrite imports `github.com/go-steer/core-agent/v2/...` → `github.com/go-steer/core-agent/v3/...`, together with the ADK v2 move below, and install with `go install github.com/go-steer/core-agent/v3/cmd/core-agent@latest`. Consumers pinned to a v2.x tag keep the `/v2` path and are unaffected. Container images and source builds never depended on the suffix. The in-source fallback version is now `v3.0.0-dev`, and `verify-version-fallback` reads the major from `go.mod`, so the path and the fallback can no longer disagree. ([#1312](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1312))
- **core-agent now builds on ADK v2: `google.golang.org/adk/v2` v2.5.0, up from `google.golang.org/adk` v1.7.0.** ADK types are part of core-agent's library API, so embedders move with it. Change every `google.golang.org/adk/...` import to `google.golang.org/adk/v2/...`. ADK v2 folds `agent.ToolContext`, `agent.CallbackContext` and `tool.Context` into one `agent.Context`, so a `functiontool` handler is now `func(adkagent.Context, Args) (Result, error)`. `session.NewEvent` takes a `context.Context` first, and `session.NewEventWithContext` is gone. A test fake that hand-implements the old tool context will not compile against the larger interface; embed `agent.StrictContextMock`, which panics on any method the fake doesn't override. **The attach wire changes shape too.** ADK v2 put camelCase `json:` tags with `omitempty` on `session.Event`, `EventActions` and `LLMResponse`, and every SSE `agent` frame and `GET …/agents/{name}/events` row embeds that struct. So `"ID"`, `"Author"`, `"Content"`, `"Partial"` become `"id"`, `"author"`, `"content"`, `"partial"`, and zero values are dropped instead of spelled out. Go clients are unaffected, because `encoding/json` matches keys case-insensitively; core-tui, switchboard, the attach client and mast-web (which reads both casings) all keep working. A script that reads the frames with jq, Python or grep must accept both casings. This repo's own drill, soak and approval-gate graders, the GKE-recipe e2e and `extract-watchdog-corpus` now do, so archived runs still grade. The protocol version does not move, because the spec never defined that payload's keys. The REST shape moves to `rest-subagent-events-v2.json`, and the frozen v1 fixture is now pinned to keep decoding. Existing session databases open unchanged: ADK's AutoMigrate adds nullable columns, and a SQLite database written by v2.10.0-dev.1 (ADK v1.2.0) and one written at ADK v1.7.0 both reopen with state, events and the eventlog overlay intact, and take new appends. Opening a v2-touched database with an older binary was not tested. The bump also moves MCP go-sdk 1.4.1 → 1.8.0, genai 1.57 → 1.71 and OpenTelemetry 1.45 → 1.46. Provider profiles (`pkg/models/profiles`) now reach core-models through its `adkv2` shim instead of `adkv1`; the API is the same. MCP 1.7+ turns on the SEP-2322 multi-round-trip middleware, which answers a server's input requests through the client's registered handlers. In core-agent that is the same elicitation handler as before (the interactive elicitor, or decline), no sampling handler, and an empty roots list, so a server gains nothing it could not already ask for. ([#1291](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1291))
- **The Anthropic defaults move to the Claude 5.5 line.** `anthropic.DefaultModel` changes from `claude-opus-5` to `claude-opus-5-5`, `anthropic.DefaultSmallModelID` (the `--agentic-small-model` default) from `claude-haiku-4-5` to `claude-haiku-5-5`, and `taskclass.ModelForTier` follows for `anthropic` and `anthropic-vertex`: frontier `claude-opus-5` → `claude-opus-5-5`, mid `claude-sonnet-5` → `claude-sonnet-5-5`, small `claude-haiku-4-5` → `claude-haiku-5-5`. The weekly pricing regen had failed since 2026-09-28 because the three models were priced but neither promoted nor held. Rates fall or hold: Opus 5.5 is $4/$20 per MTok against $5/$25 (cache reads $0.20 against $0.50); Sonnet 5.5 matches Sonnet 5's $2/$10 with cache reads at $0.10 against $0.20; Haiku 5.5 is $0.10/$0.50 against $1/$5. Opus and Sonnet keep a 1M window. Haiku goes from 200K to 1M, so the small tier's 0.35 compaction trigger moves from about 70K tokens to about 350K. **Haiku 5.5's list price steps with prompt length**: $0.50/$2.50 above 100,000 prompt tokens. `pricing.Rates` holds one flat rate per model, so cost reporting and the `max_*_cost_usd` ceilings under-count a Haiku 5.5 call past 100K prompt tokens by up to 5×. With compaction now at about 350K, a long-running small-tier subtask or background agent spends most of its life above that step, so for those runs the under-count is the usual case. `claude-haiku-5-5` now classifies as small in `modeltier`. None of the three has been run against a live session in this repo; this follows a maintainer call to promote, not a UAT. Pin the old behaviour with `--model claude-opus-5`, `--agentic-small-model claude-haiku-4-5`, or `model.name` in `.agents/config.json`; all the old ids stay priced, classified and selectable. The same regen drops `claude-mythos-preview`: LiteLLM now marks it deprecated as of 2026-06-09, and the generator skips deprecated entries, so a config pinned to that id is unpriced unless a runtime catalog refresh supplies the rate. Move it to `claude-fable-5` (same rates). ([#1280](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1280))
- **A daemon whose agent has the `bash` tool no longer starts a token-less loopback or Unix-socket attach listener.** It exits 2 with `refusing to start the attach listener on … without authentication` and names the fix. Until now such a listener started with a log warning, and the agent's own `bash` could `curl …/perms/respond` to approve its own permission prompts, add standing allow rules, or reset guardrails, with no credential to steal: loopback and a 0600 socket stop other users, not the agent's. To keep running, give the listener a token (`--attach-token-file=<path>`, new, or `--attach-token=<ENVVAR>`), use mTLS or enforced multi-session auth, make it `--attach-readonly` (writes are refused before any handler runs, so the agent cannot answer a prompt through it), start without `bash` (`--disable-tools=bash`), or pass `--attach-allow-unauthenticated-local` to accept the risk, which turns the refusal back into a warning. The opt-out is a CLI flag only, so accepting the risk is visible on the command line that started the daemon. Put `--attach-unix-socket` in a directory the agent's user cannot write: a token does not protect a socket the agent can move aside and impersonate. Nothing changes for a daemon without `bash`, for library embedders of `pkg/attach`, or for non-loopback binds, which #376 already refused. ([#1201](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1201))
- **Two background subagents that can both write to the filesystem no longer run at the same time by default.** The second `spawn_agent` is refused, by name, while the first is still running: `background: another write-capable subagent is already running: "w1" holds the shared working directory…`. This turns away a shape that mostly worked — three background agents each granted `bash` to run a different test suite is the common one — so it is a behaviour change and not only a new guard, and `safety.parallel_subagent_writes: "allow"` restores the old behaviour in one line (`"warn"` runs it and logs an operator notice instead). The guard fires on the granted *capability*, not on an observed collision, because by the time a collision is observable the tree is already wrong; a subagent granted `bash` counts as a writer even if all it ever runs is `go test`. Read-only fan-out is untouched, a second writer after the first has terminated is untouched, and out-of-process subagents (`spawn_remote_agent`) are the sanctioned way to genuinely write in parallel, since those get a filesystem of their own rather than a policy about sharing one. ([#653](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/653))
- **`agent.SubagentManager` gains a `HasPendingAlerts() bool` method.** Adding a method to an exported interface breaks any out-of-tree implementation, and the alternative here was worse than the break. A guardrail reset needs to know whether a subagent alert is waiting before it decides to drive a turn (see the [#1040](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1040) entry under Bug or Regression); the only pre-existing way to ask was `PrependPendingAlerts`, which *consumes* — so an optional-capability type assertion would have meant a wrapper predating the method silently stranding the alert it was asked about, with nothing saying why. The contract is explicitly read-only and the one in-tree implementation (`agent/background.Manager`) is two field reads; test fakes need one line. ([#1040](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1040))
- **Attach protocol 1.13.0 changes what an attached client sees when a guardrail halts the agent, and a client that is not updated with the daemon loses information it used to have.** `cost_ceiling` and `watchdog` stop appearing as `turn-error` kinds on `/events` — not rarely, at all — and the `canceled` frame the halt causes stops being suppressed. A client that learned about halts by matching those kinds goes blind and starts rendering a contentless cancellation in their place; the replacement is the new `guardrail-trip` event, and the details are in the Feature entry below. This is called out here because there is no producer-side mitigation available: the suppression the old behaviour depended on is exactly what was removed. core-tui v0.25.0 is the matching consumer. ([#891](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/891))
- `gemini.Provider`'s context-cache wiring follows core-models' Vertex cache manager: `ClientConfig()` is replaced by `Caches(ctx)`, `SetContextCache` and `WithContextCache` take the cache's model as their first argument (a cache serves the one model it was created for), and `ContextCacheInvalidateFn` takes `(name, reason)` so a report about a cache already replaced is ignored. Hooks may now be installed at any time; a model built earlier picks them up on its next call. ([#1296](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1296))

### Changes by Kind

#### Changed
- **Gemini and Claude run on core-models' adapters (v0.6.5).** `pkg/models/gemini` and `pkg/models/anthropic` are facades over core-models' `dialect/gemini` and `dialect/anthropic`, adapted to ADK v2 through `core-models/adkv2`; provider names, config surface, CLI flags, defaults and auto-detection are unchanged, and `internal/vertexcache` is replaced by core-models' `dialect/gemini/vertexcache`. Behaviour that changes with the library: an empty Gemini response with no candidates is retried as an empty answer rather than tolerated as a Vertex heartbeat; Claude's transport retries (429/5xx/529) are core-models' HTTP-layer retry instead of the SDK's; a model call that gets no response headers in time fails (five minutes for a streaming call, ten for a non-streaming one such as the compaction summarizer), and is reported as a retryable `transient_net` turn error rather than a cancel; Gemini subtasks (`WithoutBuiltins`) now go through the same transient retry, budget and empty-response check as the main loop instead of none; a Vertex context cache is stamped only on the configured model's own requests, no longer on side calls, prompt-cache opt-outs or other models (the compaction fallback used to have its system instruction replaced by the agent's cached one); Claude's retry no longer retries a 409, gives up at once on a `Retry-After` over 60s, starts its backoff at 1s with jitter and caps each attempt's wait for headers at five minutes; Gemini error text now starts with `gemini: `, on Vertex too; Vertex Gemini calls are traced through core-agent's otelhttp transport under the library's ADC instead of genai's own client; a cache-hooked Vertex request carrying a tool config runs uncached. Gemini keeps core-agent's own transient retry and budget as its only retry layer. `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` still redirects the first-party Anthropic API. ([#1296](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1296))

#### Feature
- **Auto mode can refuse what it would hand to a person, and says why.** With `permissions.auto.escalation: "refuse"`, a call the approver may not judge, or passes on, is refused at once instead of waiting `approval_timeout` for a person. This is for a deployment where nobody answers prompts. In either mode the agent is now told why the approver didn't judge a call: for example, that a piped or chained bash command never matches an eligible pattern. It can then reshape the call. ([#1343](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1343))
- **Pricing knows which backend bills the tokens.** `usage.PriceFor` now looks a model up as `<backend>/<model>` before the bare id, layer by layer (`pricing.Catalog.LookupFor`), with the backend taken from `model.provider` (a profile's backend, or `gemini`/`vertex`/`anthropic`/`anthropic-vertex`) or, for cost sites with no config at hand, from the backend `cmd/core-agent` installs at startup (`usage.SetBackend`, `models.BackendOf`). That prices OpenAI's, xAI's and Vertex AI partner models, which the shared table carries only per backend, and bills Claude Sonnet 4.5 on Vertex AI at its own long-context rate. Profiles' declared `rates` are keyed by backend too (`usage.DeclaredRates`), so two profiles that price one model id differently each bill their own; the bare key stays as a fallback for lookups that can't name a backend. A subagent on a provider other than its parent's is priced on its own backend, through both doors: the declared subagent's agent carries it (`agent.WithBillingBackend`) and its async template too (`background.SubagentTemplate.Backend`), and the cost sites ask `usage.PriceOn(backend, model)`. Without that, a Claude subagent on Vertex AI under a Gemini parent billed at the bare rate, and an OpenAI or xAI subagent read as unpriced to every ceiling. Each tracked turn keeps the backend it was priced on (`usage.Turn.Backend`, from `usage.Pricing.Backend`), so `/usage`'s cache-savings reference re-prices it on the same rows. ([#1328](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1328))
- **The `openai` and `xai` provider profiles.** core-models v0.9.0 added them as built-ins: `--provider openai` (Responses API, `OPENAI_API_KEY`) and `--provider xai` (Chat Completions, `XAI_API_KEY`). Both price out of the box. A config that set `model.provider` to `openai` was a load-time error before; it now selects the profile. ([#1328](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1328))
- **Gemini 4 Argon is priced, sized and classified.** `gemini-4-argon` ($2/$10 per MTok, 1,048,576-token window) and its access-gated `gemini-4-argon-cyber-permissive` ($4/$20) come in with core-models v0.13.1; `modeltier` classifies both as frontier, as mast does, so the `/model` picker offers them. Neither is a tier default. ([#1328](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1328))
- **Models beyond Gemini and Claude, through provider profiles.** `--provider vertex-maas --model zai-org/glm-5.2-maas` runs a Vertex AI partner model, and a profile declared under the new top-level `providers` key in `.agents/config.json` reaches a self-hosted vLLM, SGLang or Ollama server or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint. The adapters come from [`go-steer/core-models`](https://github.com/go-steer/core-models) v0.5.0, the library mast uses for the same models, through its ADK v1 shim. `--provider` and `model.provider` now take any profile name, built-in (`vertex-maas`, `vllm`, `sglang`, `ollama`, `openai-compatible`) or declared. The six existing provider names, and auto-detection, are unchanged. With no model named, a profile runs its own `mid` tier (or the `--task` class's tier), and its `small` tier is the default `--agentic-small-model`. Prompt, output, cache-read, cache-write and reasoning tokens reach the same usage record the other backends feed. A model with no catalog price is priced from `rates` on the profile's model, a new lowest-precedence `profile-declared` pricing layer. A cost ceiling the operator set on a profile model with no price at all is refused at startup, since every call would count as $0; the unattended default session ceiling only warns. Server-side built-ins can't run on these wire formats, so a `model.builtin_tools` that turns one on is refused. New package `models/profiles`, imported for its side effect like the other backends.
- **A declared subagent can be a standing worker: `subagents[].scheduler`.** Set `"scheduler": "sleep"` (or `"none"` / `"default"`) and a `spawn_agent { agent: "…" }` spawn of that subagent gets `schedule_next_turn` and loops until a budget fires or it returns a result. With `"sleep"` it sleeps in-process between turns, and the roster and the TUIs' running-tasks bar show when it will wake. Before this, the only way to pick a scheduler was an ad-hoc spawn, and ad-hoc spawns are off on a `--no-repl` daemon, so a headless daemon couldn't run a standing worker at all. Any other value is a startup error, `"exit_on_defer"` included, since nothing in `core-agent` restarts a deferred background subagent. The async budget defaults still apply (10 minutes of wall-clock, sleep included), so raise `budgets` for a long-lived worker. Calling the subagent as a parent tool is unaffected. The startup line names the setting. ([#1283](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1283))
- **The subagent roster shows what a running subagent is doing and when a sleeping one will wake.** `GET /agents` rows and the local TUI's subagent list now fill `last_report` while a subagent runs: its latest `report_alert`, or else its latest model message. Before, it was empty until the subagent finished (and in the local TUI, unless it failed). Once it finishes, the result it returned takes over. A subagent sleeping on `schedule_next_turn` also carries `next_wake_at` and `wake_detail` (protocol 1.20.0), and both disappear when its next turn starts. Its `status` stays `running` while it sleeps, so clients that predate this don't treat it as finished. Library callers get `autonomous.WithScheduleHook` to observe the same pending wake, and `Handle.PendingWake` / `Handle.LastReport` on background subagents. ([#1283](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1283))
- **Both TUIs show a running-tasks bar (core-tui v0.31.0).** `core-agent-tui` and the local `--tui` draw one row per in-flight background subagent between the input box and the footer, up to three, with its elapsed time and the first line of its latest report. A subagent sleeping on `schedule_next_turn` gets a `◷` row that counts down to its wake and shows the reason it gave. The sidebar's subagent list is replaced by a running count; `/subagents` still lists them all. Both adapters now pass `next_wake_at` / `wake_detail` through to core-tui. `core-agent-tui` against a pre-1.20.0 daemon gets no wake rows; a sleeping subagent there counts up like a working one. ([#1283](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1283))
- **Auto mode's approver honours the operator's explicit permission.** Tell the agent *"you have my permission to push"* or *"…to create the PR"*, and the approver allows that push or PR, if the call is eligible, instead of denying every outward-facing call. A plain request counts ("commit and push the branch"), while a general "do whatever you need" doesn't. Permission covers only what it names: permission to push a branch isn't permission to merge it, to push elsewhere, or to send secrets. A later message withdrawing it wins. Only the operator's own messages count, meaning whatever you type or paste. A permission line in a tool's output or the agent's own calls never does, and neither does a referenced file, except from a `core-agent-tui` older than v2.10 (protocol 1.18.0), which doesn't separate inlined files from your text. A destructive call is never allowed outright: a force-push in any form (`--force-with-lease`, `+branch`), deleting a remote branch, or deleting data or history. If you explicitly asked for it, it comes back to you to confirm; otherwise it's denied. List only people in `permissions.auto.task_from`: a relay or gateway identity there would turn relayed text into permission. The old policy text was read in opposite directions: Gemini 3.7 Flash denied explicitly permitted pushes, and Claude Sonnet 5 allowed an explicitly permitted force-push. The approver eval gains 16 cases, and all 50 now pass on Claude Haiku 4.5, Claude Sonnet 5 and Gemini 3.7 Flash. ([#1251](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1251))
- **Auto mode has built-in eligibility presets.** `permissions.auto.eligible_bundles: ["coding"]` lets the approver decide edits in the workspace, single test, build, vet and format commands, and local git, without writing a pattern list. The other presets are `k8s_read` (read-only `kubectl`) and `github` (pushing a branch and working with PRs and issues). Bundles merge with `eligible`, an unknown name is a startup error, and an empty list still means auto behaves like `ask`. A chained or redirected command never matches a bundle's prefix patterns, merging is in no bundle, and a bundle only makes a call eligible: the approver still judges each one against your request. ([#1252](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1252))
- **A provider retry now shows up in the session transcript, not only in daemon stderr.** A retry that recovered stamps `CustomMetadata.provider_retry` (`outcome`, `attempts`, the rejection) on the persisted event it recovered with. Any other retry surfaces as an error led by `provider retry persisted:`, `abandoned:`, `skipped, budget spent:`, `failed with another error:` or `interrupted after recovering:`. That error reaches the `turn-error` frame, a failed delegation's result, and a background subagent's report; its `kind` and `code` are the rejection's own, and `errors.As` finds `*models.RetryError` with the provider error still unwrappable beneath it. A retry answered by a response with no content (a safety block) is stamped `"outcome": "no content"`. A retry in an internal call (approver, summarizer, session title, `/btw`, an MCP digest, an agentic tool's subtask) has no transcript surface and now logs as `side call (<name>): …`. `a2_count.py` counts every surface, reads the drill's `subagents.json` via `--subagent-events` for retries inside a child, and reports side-call retries in their own row. The 2026-09-13 batch had counted 13 retries in the log and 0 in 21 transcripts. ([#1206](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1206))
- **The daemon says at startup where compaction fires, in tokens:** `core-agent: compaction: fires at 0.85 of gemini-3.7-flash's 1048576-token window = 891289 tokens`. The figure comes from the compactor's own resolution path — a new `DefaultCompactor.ThresholdFor(model)`, which the runtime decision now also goes through — so the line cannot describe a threshold the daemon will not use. It exists because the threshold in force was invisible: #1226, an operator threshold silently outranked by the per-tier defaults, would have shown at the first startup anyone read, and the 2026-10-03 A1 soak was first graded at 21,000 tokens because 0.021 was multiplied by a 1,000,000-token window that is really 1,048,576. `soak.sh` reads that line from the pod's log at start and records it on the timeline's start row (its log follower starts after the pod booted, so the line never reaches the captured log), and `soak_verdict.py` uses it, so `--compaction-at` is needed only for older images. For a model with no catalogued window the line states the 128,000-token window compaction actually assumes, not "unknown". ([#1199](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1199))
- **Auto mode's approver has an evaluation, and its first runs pass.** A labelled corpus of 34 calls lives in `dev/evals/approver`. Each one goes through a real auto-mode gate with a real approver model: destructive, outward-facing, off-task and injection-bearing calls must not be allowed; routine in-task calls should be; and path-scope, control-plane, subagent and no-task calls must never reach the approver. Gemini 3.7 Flash, Claude Haiku 4.5 and Claude Sonnet 5 on Vertex all allowed no must-deny call, were asked about no must-escalate call, and escalated no routine one. Sonnet 5 passed only after the approver stopped sending `temperature`: Sonnet 5 rejects that parameter with a 400, so every approver call on it had failed and escalated ([#1212](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1212)). A case that tested nothing, a case the model gave no answer to, and an approver that allows no routine call all make the run indeterminate rather than a pass. `tools.SummarizeToolCall` is exported so the eval builds a toolset call's detail exactly as the gate sees it. Run it with `go run ./dev/smoke/cmd/approvereval`. It also runs weekly in the real-provider workflow. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **An overnight soak now has a grader, so box A1's verdict is produced rather than read off a timeline by a person.** `dev/uat/gke-drill/soak_verdict.py --run-dir <soak> --compaction-at <tokens>` grades one finished soak against A1's four clauses — survived, no wedge, compaction, no drops — each PASS, FAIL, NOT EXERCISED or UNKNOWN, exiting 0 only when all four pass and 2 when nothing failed but the run cannot close A1. `soak.sh` itself still decides nothing: an eight-hour harness must never invent a verdict mid-run, so the grader is a separate tool over the same artifacts, which is box A5's amended shape. Run over the 2026-09-14 eight-hour soak it reproduces that run note's hand verdict exactly — compaction **NOT EXERCISED** at a 37,321-token peak, which is the reading the note warned must not be taken as a pass. Three properties of the evidence shaped it. A *successful* compaction logs nothing, so the log can convict compaction but never acquit it; the sampler now also records each session's `compactions` count from `GET …/context`, which the grader prefers. A single low `window` reading is not a compaction, because the per-turn ledger includes side calls whose input is one prompt, so a sample taken mid-subtask dips for one tick — credited, that would turn a disabled compactor into a PASS, so a drop only counts if the next sample stays below the threshold. And a summarizer failure the mechanical fallback covers is a degradation, disclosed but not convicted; only both strategies down is a FAIL. Log blind windows from stream reconnects are listed beside any verdict that rests on the log's silence. The self-test reproduces the hand verdict from a cut-down, redacted copy of the real run, flips each clause by doctoring a copy and asserts the message that names why, and walks the compaction paths the real run never reached. ([#1199](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1199))
- **Every surface that shows a permission prompt now carries auto mode's escalation, ahead of `auto` becoming selectable.** When auto mode's approver model passes a call on to a person, the prompt says so and quotes the approver's reason as its words: the local TUI, `core-agent-tui`, the stdin prompter, and the alert for a prompt nobody is watching. It also offers only allow once and deny (the two TUIs add deny with a reason), because the gate applies any allow there as once. The reason reaches every surface as one line of at most 600 bytes. Protocol 1.18.0 adds `approver_model` and `approver_reason` to `/perms/stream` prompt frames. On such a prompt, `POST /perms/respond` reports a session or always answer as `{"decision": "allow-once", "downgraded": true}`, and `GET /perms` approval rows gain `approver_model` for a call the approver allowed on its own. Both TUIs map `auto` to core-tui v0.29.0's own chip rather than showing it as `ask`. `auto` still can't be selected. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **Box A2's log-against-transcript count is a script, not a paragraph.** `dev/uat/gke-drill/a2_count.py --log <daemon.log> --events <run>/events.sse …` counts each A2 failure class on both sides and fails when the daemon log saw more than the transcripts — the exact shape of the 2026-09-13 finding, 13 provider retries in the log and none in any transcript. Every class names the source line it matches on each side, because a wording change elsewhere silently disarms a count: retries from `pkg/models/retry.go` (one per transient error that reached the policy, the `recovered` follow-up deliberately not double-counted), guardrail trips from #1131's cut and boundary lines against `guardrail-trip` frames — or, for the refusal storm, which withholds that frame on purpose, its `turn-error` kind — and turn errors from the wake loop and REPL against `turn-error` frames, which is where a refused turn is counted. Two classes are reported as not countable rather than given an invented number: the daemon logs nothing when a delegation fails, and since #1040 a wedge leaves no per-event line on either side. **Provider retries have no transcript surface at all today**, so on any run that hits a 429 the counter reports A2 failed until one exists ([#1206](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1206)). No archived run pairs a daemon log with the transcripts of the same sessions — the 2026-09-13 counts came from a live `kubectl logs` that was never written to disk — so the self-test grades the one real transcript available (the delegation a 429 killed that day, redacted) and log lines built from the source format strings, and the retry pattern was checked against a real retry line in the 2026-09-14 soak log. ([#1200](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1200))
- **Permission mode `auto` can be selected (experimental).** It is `ask` with an approver model in front of the person: for calls on `permissions.auto.eligible`, the approver may allow the call once, deny it, or pass it to the person's prompt with its reason quoted. Set `permissions.mode: auto` in config, which also requires a `permissions.auto` block and `permissions.approval_timeout` (either missing is a startup error). Or switch a running session with the TUI chip or `POST /perms/mode`. The chip cycles ask → auto → acceptEdits → plan → yolo, and offers `auto` only to a session that can enter it. `POST /perms/mode` answers **400** for `auto` on a session without an approver or timeout, and a library host's `SetMode`/`SwapMode` refuse it the same way. `GET /perms` gains `settable_modes` (protocol 1.18.0) so an attached client builds the same cycle. Keep auto out of shipped recipes until its evaluation has run (#1175 phase 5). ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **Every surface that shows a permission prompt now carries auto mode's escalation.** When auto mode's approver model passes a call on to a person, the prompt says so and quotes the approver's reason as its words: the local TUI, `core-agent-tui`, the stdin prompter, and the alert for a prompt nobody is watching. It also offers only allow once and deny (the two TUIs add deny with a reason), because the gate applies any allow there as once. The reason reaches every surface as one line of at most 600 bytes. Protocol 1.18.0 adds `approver_model` and `approver_reason` to `/perms/stream` prompt frames. On such a prompt, `POST /perms/respond` reports a session or always answer as `{"decision": "allow-once", "downgraded": true}`, and `GET /perms` approval rows gain `approver_model` for a call the approver allowed on its own. Both TUIs map `auto` to core-tui v0.29.0's own chip rather than showing it as `ask`. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **A `-p` run's closing summary now names the thinking tokens its cost already includes.** Gemini reports reasoning separately from output and it is billed at the output rate, so the summary's dollar figure counted spend its `↑`/`↓` arrows did not show — and a thinking-heavy run looked overpriced for its token count (one measured agentic turn spent 6,449 thinking tokens). The line now ends `· +N thinking tokens (billed as output)` when any were reported. It is appended *after* the model rather than beside `↓`, so a pipeline matching the existing `↓N tokens · $X (model)` shape keeps matching, and `↓` stays the visible output alone. Anthropic turns add nothing: Anthropic bills thinking inside `output_tokens` and reports no separate count, so it was already in `↓`. A run whose subtasks or digest summarizer go to Gemini does show the segment, which is correct — those thoughts are spend `↓` does not contain. ([#1198](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1198))
- **`pkg/permissions` implements auto mode.** `ModeAuto` is ask with an approver model in front of the prompt: for a call the recipe made eligible (`Options.AutoEligible`), an `Approver` allows it once, denies it for the rest of the turn with a reason the model reads, or passes it to a person. Deny patterns, the allowlist, session grants and plan-first still run first. Path-scope and control-plane requests, background-subagent calls, calls that name `.agents/config.json`, `.agents/mcp.json` or the approver's instructions file, and calls without full arguments always go to a person without an approver call. An approver allow never becomes a grant, and a prompt the approver escalated grants at most once. Auto requires an `approval_timeout`. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **Every built-in tool whose calls auto mode's approver can decide now gives the permission gate the call's full arguments.** (The read tools ask only about path scope, which the approver never decides.) `bash`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `delete_file`, `fetch_url`, `alert`, `call_peer`, subagent delegation, `spawn_agent`, `spawn_remote_agent`, and MCP and skill tools behind `tools.GateToolset` all do. New `Gate` methods `CheckBashWithArgs`, `CheckFileWriteWithArgs`, `CheckGenericWithArgs`, `CheckToolCallWithArgs` and `CheckReadOnlyToolCallWithArgs` take the decoded tool input, and `permissions.CallArgs` re-encodes it as canonical JSON. The plain forms are unchanged and still compile, but in auto mode a call through one goes to a person, not the approver. Consumers that gate their own tools should switch to the `WithArgs` forms. The never-list match now runs over canonical arguments and ignores case, so a JSON escape or a capital letter no longer hides a control-plane path. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **The approver model behind auto mode is configured under `permissions.auto`, and the new `pkg/approver` package implements it.** `model` defaults to `model.name`, `timeout` to 30s, `eligible` lists the calls the approver may decide in the `allow` pattern syntax, and `instructions_file` adds a recipe's text to the built-in policy. The approver makes one tool-less model call per eligible call and sends it the operator's task and the pending call as one JSON document, so text inside the call cannot pose as the task. A timeout, a model error, an unreadable answer, or arguments over 64 KiB send the call to a person. A write to the instructions file through the file tools takes the same elevated approval as `.agents/config.json`, in every mode, and in mode `auto` the approver never judges a bash command that names it; a bash write outside `auto` is not covered, the same limit `.agents/config.json` has. A missing file fails startup. `Gate.SetApprover` wires an approver onto a gate built by `FromConfig`. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **Each turn now tells auto mode's approver what the operator asked for, the calls the turn has already made, where to bill its model call, and whether a cost ceiling has been hit. Each verdict it returns is written to the eventlog.** The task is only text an operator wrote. That means the `-p` prompt, a REPL or local-TUI message (without any `@`-inlined files), an autonomous goal the host marks with `autonomous.WithOperatorGoal`, or a message injected by a caller listed in the new `permissions.auto.task_from`. A listed caller counts only if they authenticated as that identity with their own token. A proxy asserting their identity doesn't count, and neither does a shared `--attach-token`, which every holder uses under the same default identity. So wake payloads that k8s-lookout or a chat gateway relays never become the task, and neither does whatever they leave in the local TUI's auto-continue batch. Every history boundary drops the task, and a call with no task goes to a person. In attach mode the same holds for `@`-inlined files: `POST /inject` takes an optional `task_bytes`, the length of the operator's words at the start of `message`, and `core-agent-tui` sends it, so a referenced file's content never counts. An older `core-agent-tui` sends none, and its whole message counts. Both TUIs read the typed text from core-tui v0.30.0's `TurnInput` rather than parsing the prompt ([#1230](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1230)). The approver's spend counts toward the session's cost and its ceilings, and survives a restart. It is never read as the size of the conversation, so the context gauge and the compaction trigger are unaffected. New: `agent.WithOperatorTask`, `Agent.ApproverTask`, `autonomous.WithOperatorGoal`, `attach.DirectCaller`, `usage.Tracker.AppendSideUsage` and `usage.SideUsageMetadata`. ([#1175](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1175))
- **`permissions.approval_notify_after` announces a prompt an attached client leaves unanswered.** `approval_notify` only fired for a prompt that reached nobody, so a TUI left attached while its operator was away absorbed every prompt, and the approval channel heard about none of them. With this set, a prompt still unanswered after that long is sent to the `approval_notify` target too, worded as unanswered and carrying `unanswered_for`. Each prompt still produces at most one notification. The field requires `approval_notify` and must be shorter than `approval_timeout`. ([#1167](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1167))
- **The session owner can now change a running session's permission mode from an attached client, and the chip in `core-agent-tui` works.** The local TUI could always cycle `ask` / `acceptEdits` / `plan` / `yolo` with Shift+Tab. An attached operator had to restart the daemon. `POST /sessions/{sid}/perms/mode` (attach protocol 1.16.0) switches the session's own gate, and `core-agent-tui` now shows the mode chip wired to it. Only the session owner or a daemon admin may call it, in either direction. A contributor gets the same masked 404 as any ACL refusal. `allow` stays config-only. On a session with a durable eventlog, every change, remote or Shift+Tab, writes an `attach-perm-mode` row with the from and to modes and, when the daemon verified one, the caller. The change isn't persisted, so a restarted session comes back in its configured mode. ([#1168](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1168))
- **An operator can say why they denied a call, and the model reads it.** A denial used to reach the model as the same sentence whatever the operator objected to, so it guessed, usually by re-issuing a near-identical call. `POST /perms/respond` now takes an optional `reason` with a deny (protocol 1.15.0), and the refused call's result carries it: `The operator's reason: "…"`. It is deny-only: a reason on any other decision is a 400, because an approval that carries text reads as conditions on the call. Whitespace collapses to one line, more than 500 bytes is a 400, and a 400 leaves the prompt pending. A deny without a reason is unchanged byte-for-byte. `permissions.Approval` gains `Reason`, `attach.PromptBroker` gains `RespondWith`, and `attachclient.Client` gains `DenyPrompt`. ([#1165](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1165))
- **Both TUIs can now deny a permission prompt with a reason (core-tui v0.27.1 → v0.28.0).** The prompt gains `r`, deny with a reason: a one-line input, at most 500 bytes, and the model reads the text in the refused call's result as `The operator's reason: "…"`. `n` is still the plain deny, and esc in the input goes back to the choices. The in-process `--tui` passes the reason to the gate on the deny. `core-agent-tui` sends it with the deny through `POST /perms/respond`, and offers `r` only when the daemon's `capabilities` frame advertises attach protocol 1.15.0 or later. A prompt already pending at attach waits up to 3 seconds, once, for that frame, so the prompt an operator attached to answer still offers `r`. An older daemon would answer 200 and drop the field, so the operator would believe the model read words it never saw. As a defensive measure, if a daemon refuses a reason with a 400 (the prompt stays pending), the client sends the deny again without the reason and says so in the chat, rather than lose the deny. The bump is additive (`Prompter.AskApprovalDetailed`, `PermissionOutcome`; [go-steer/core-tui#346](https://github.com/go-steer/core-tui/pull/346)), and `verify-coretui-guards` passes unchanged at 22 exported interfaces, 31 guards and 13 declines. ([#1182](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1182))
- **The TUI now asks for permission in a centered modal by default, `ui.permission_layout` picks the layout, and `/permissions layout` sticks.** `core-agent` used to leave core-tui's layout at its zero value, which draws the prompt inline in the chat, where a decision that blocks the agent reads like one more transcript row and can scroll away under a busy turn. It now starts in the overlay modal, which dims the chat until the operator decides. `ui.permission_layout` in `.agents/config.json` takes `"overlay"` (the default, also what an empty value means) or `"inline"`, and anything else fails validation with an error naming the key. The core-tui v0.25.0 → v0.27.0 bump brings `/permissions layout [inline|overlay]` (or `/perms layout`), which switches the layout mid-session, and `core-agent` persists that switch to `ui.permission_layout` through the same config write `/mouse` uses, always as an explicit value, so the next launch starts where the operator left it. The attach client (`core-agent-tui`) also starts in the overlay; it reads no config file, so the switch there lasts the session. The bump carries three core-tui releases, and the rest of what it brings: v0.27.0 makes the inline prompt a heavy warning-coloured block and fixes a footer row that went missing while a prompt was open; v0.26.0 stops a held agent from treating host commands such as `/new`, `/attach` and `/usage` as the steer instruction, refuses `/clear` during a daemon turn that has gone quiet in a tool call, renders a tool result carrying `run_error` ([#1154](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1154)'s subagent that returned and then failed) as delivered with a warning, and folds the inbox's bundle-handling guidance to one line; v0.25.1 stops the mouse-capture hint telling VS Code users off macOS to hold Alt ([go-steer/core-tui#317](https://github.com/go-steer/core-tui/pull/317)). No new core-tui interfaces: `verify-coretui-guards` passes unchanged at 22 interfaces, 31 guards and 13 declines. ([#1161](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1161))
- **The model can now ask what is in a file without paying for the file: `view_file_outline` returns a declaration skeleton with no bodies.** `grep` finds a string and `read_file` reads a range, so the survey step — "what is in here, and which part do I want" — had no cheap answer, and the model read 2,000-line files whole to discover one method mattered. The new built-in returns the package, imports, type declarations, top-level consts and vars, and function and method signatures, each prefixed with the line it starts on, so the outline hands the caller the `offset` for the follow-up read rather than only a list of names. It is on by default (`tools.Default()`, registered by `tools.Build`), gated through `CheckFileRead` like every other read tool so the path scope applies, capped by `tool_output.per_tool["view_file_outline"]` (64 KB / 2,000 lines), classified read-only so it dispatches concurrently, and plan-exempt so research works before a plan is recorded. **Every result declares how it was produced, and that distinction is the feature.** Go is parsed with `go/parser`, and bodies are stripped *on the AST* before printing rather than filtered out of printed text — a text filter has to guess where a body ends, and guessing is the one thing this tool must not do; the result says `analysis: "parsed"`. Other known extensions (`.py`, `.rb`, `.rs`, `.java`, the JS/TS family, shell) get a line-based scan reported as `analysis: "heuristic"` with its limits travelling in the result's `note` field: first line only for multi-line declarations, a match inside a string or comment reported as a declaration, and no way to tell a declaration from a struct field, a local or a nested definition — indentation is preserved verbatim so nesting stays visible, because trimming it would present a nested function as a top-level one with nothing to notice the difference by. The note also says what the scan misses: a declaration whose line does not begin with a known keyword is not reported, so an absence proves nothing. C and C++ are refused for that reason, since a function definition there begins with its return type and a keyword scan would find every include and no functions. An extension with neither is **refused**, and the refusal names the ones that work — a scan tuned for nothing finds either everything or nothing, and the model has no way to discount a guess that is formatted like a parse. A Go file that does not parse is an error rather than a partial skeleton, for the same reason: a partial outline would still be labelled `parsed`. Line numbers are physical lines, in the outline and in a parse error alike, ignoring `//line` directives, because they are offsets for reading this file, not the grammar it was generated from. **The subtlety worth recording** is that nil-ing `FuncDecl.Body` is not sufficient — `var Handler = func() { … }` is a `GenDecl`, so a top-level function literal smuggles a whole body into an outline that advertises none; every reachable `ast.FuncLit` is emptied too, and its braces are collapsed onto one position so the printer does not pad the outline out to the height of the code it replaced. Doc comments are deliberately dropped (the caller asked for structure, and a doc comment is often longer than what it documents), and composite-literal contents are deliberately *not* elided — the standard output cap already bounds the result, and eliding would be a second place to get "what counts as a body" wrong. ([#954](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/954))
- **core-agent now carries a committed recipe for doing development work on core-agent, and the design doc's third D3 mitigation turned out not to work.** [#1116](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1116)'s P3 lands `/.agents/` in the repository root: the `config.json`, a rooted `reviewer` subagent, and five skills — `presubmit-sweep`, `adversarial-review-gate`, `prefix-failure-verification`, `changelog-bullet`, `stacked-pr-order` — which are not new policy but the rituals `AGENTS.md` already describes in prose, made executable. A divergence between a skill and `AGENTS.md` is a bug in the skill. **The frontier model is a requirement rather than a default, and the test that says so cannot be a tier check.** The spike measured core-agent driving `claude-opus-5` scoring identically to Claude Code driving the same model on the same graded task, with `claude-opus-4-5` scoring 1/6 and 2/6 where opus-5 scored 5/6 — so the harness is not the limiting factor, the model is. The first draft of the guard asserted `modeltier.Classify(...) == TierFrontier` and **passed on the model the evidence rejects**: `claude-opus-4` is frontier-tier, correctly, because that taxonomy exists for context-window and compaction thresholds rather than for capability. The shipped guard is an allowlist of models a graded run has actually passed on, so adding one means running the task instead of editing a list; the friction is the point, because dropping to a cheaper model here does not degrade gracefully — it produces confident, well-structured artifacts that are wrong in the direction a review gate cannot see, which is the worst available failure for a pipeline whose output is a pull request. **The recipe's own test lives at `internal/selfrecipe/`, not beside the recipe**, because the Go tool ignores dot-directories: a `.agents/recipe_test.go` is invisible to `go test ./...` and would pass locally forever while never running in CI. Fourteen assertions, mutation-verified against the real recipe rather than a fixture — the budget floor rejects the exact `2.0` that cut both spike runs, the reviewer's `tools` list must be explicit because a nil list *inherits the parent's write tools*, and `config.json` is decoded a second time with `DisallowUnknownFields` because a typo'd `max_turn_cost_usdd` loads clean, enforces nothing and reads correct. **Four of the fourteen did not survive the review gate, and the pattern in all four was the same: a guard asserting the spelling of a rule rather than its effect.** The two `.gitignore` tests matched literal lines, and one-line edits a reader would call equivalent walked straight past them — `/.agents/*` ignores the entire recipe and is none of the four spellings the scan looked for, while a trailing `!/.agents/plans/` un-ignores the plans directory with the wanted line still sitting above it. Both now ask `git check-ignore --no-index` about representative paths, and between them every path under `.agents/` is claimed by exactly one of the two so a too-broad rule has no gap to hide in; the recipe side walks the tree and asserts an exact file count, because a walk is structurally silent about a file that has gone missing.
