- **`AGENTS.md` gains the conventions that were living only in an assistant's private memory, and one of them was actively wrong.** Eight durable lessons moved into the repo, where any contributor and any agent can read them: the `cmd/core-agent` wiring rule that anything resolved before `runner.Run` must take a getter rather than a value off `agentRef` (an eager read is a startup SIGSEGV even with the feature off, shipped once as [#647](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/647)), the third stacked-PR gotcha where a base update duplicates the CHANGELOG version heading without ever conflicting, that "No checks reported" on a PR means `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY` rather than broken CI, and how to prove a branch is already in main before deleting it. The correction matters more than the additions: the pre-fix verification recipe told you to run the new test against the parent commit in a scratch checkout, which does not work when the fix introduced the symbols the test names — the method is to patch the *behaviour* back with `// PREFIX BEHAVIOUR` markers and predict the failure list first. The shell-scanner bullet is corrected too, and the spike is what caught it: it asserted that over-blanking is always the safe direction, which holds only for a check that fails when it cannot find something. For a violation scanner the directions invert, and reading the bullet as written led one spike arm to reuse a lexer that blanks quoted spans for a check hunting `"${CORE_AGENT}"` — a gate that would have found nothing in any script, forever. Also recorded: `git -c tag.gpgsign=false` and the release-notes dry run that exits 128 having written a full-size file and printed nothing, and that `set-up-demo.sh` leaves your checkout dirty with real cluster coordinates in tracked files. ([#1116](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1116))

#### Security
- **Builds on Go 1.26.10, for GO-2026-6809.** A `crypto/x509` certificate-verification vulnerability in go1.26.9 is reachable through the attach server's HTTP stack. `go.mod`'s `toolchain` line, the Dockerfiles and the release images move to go1.26.10. The `go 1.26.6` floor for embedders is unchanged, so an embedder building on an older toolchain should upgrade it. ([#1346](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1346))
- **`--attach-client-ca` without `--attach-tls-cert` / `--attach-tls-key` no longer counts as authentication.** Without a server cert the client CA is never loaded and the listener speaks plain HTTP. Even so, the CA alone satisfied the rule that a non-loopback listener must be authenticated, so `--attach-listen=:7777 --attach-client-ca=ca.pem` exposed an unauthenticated listener to the network. That configuration is now refused like any other unauthenticated non-loopback bind. ([#1201](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1201))
- **The attach token can come from a file, so it never sits in any process's environment.** New: `--attach-token-file=<path>` (config `attach.token_file`) on the daemon, and `--token-file=<path>` on `core-agent-tui`, `core-agent attach` and `core-agent ls`. The file is read once at startup, and the process then makes itself non-dumpable on Linux, because the token is now in its memory. This closes two routes `--attach-token` / `--token-env` could not: the token `export`ed in the shell that launched the daemon, and the milliseconds between a client starting and taking its token, a window an agent already running on the machine could win. A pipe works and leaves nothing on disk: `--token-file <(pass show attach | head -n1)`. Each file and env source is mutually exclusive with the other, and naming both is an error. A regular file that any other user can access, or that the group can write, is refused, so for a Kubernetes secret volume set `defaultMode: 0400`, or `0440` with `fsGroup`. The trailing newline is trimmed. The file is never deleted. Agent file tools are refused on it in every mode and an instruction file cannot `@include` it, and a `bash` command that names it directly is refused too. But `bash` can still reach a regular file by other spellings, so a regular one is fully out of the agent's reach only if the agent's user cannot read that path (see the credential-files entry below). ([#1201](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1201))
- **Agent tools can no longer read or rewrite the multi-session bearer table, and a root daemon whose children would hold `CAP_SYS_PTRACE` now says so at startup.** In multi-session mode the attach credential is `attach.multi_session.auth.table_file`. It is a plaintext file the daemon's user can read, and the agent runs as that user, so file modes do nothing. A model that read the table could answer its own permission prompts. One that wrote it could add its own token for the next boot. Every gate built from a config that names the table now refuses all agent file tools on it, in every mode including yolo: `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `delete_file`, `grep`, `glob`, `read_many_files`, `json_query`, `view_file_outline`, `list_dir` and `stat`. A path matches if it is the table *now*, so symlinks, hard links and the new generation after a Kubernetes Secret update are all caught. An operator cannot approve the refusal, and directory walks skip the file. Instruction files can no longer pull it in either. An agent with nothing but `write_file` could have added `@include users.json` to `AGENTS.md` and received every token in the next session's prompt; that load now fails and names the file. The table is protected whenever it is configured, even with multi-session switched off. The attach token file gets the same protection (`attach.token_file`, `${VAR}`-expanded, or `--attach-token-file`). A `bash` command that names the file directly is refused too, but that is a seatbelt, not a boundary: `cd` and a bare name, a glob or a variable reach it, and only the operating system can stop that. So startup now warns whenever the daemon's user can read the table and `bash` is registered or the table sits inside the agent's path scope. The warning names the two fixes: disable `bash` on that daemon, or make the file unreadable to its user once loaded. **Behaviour change:** hook commands go through the same `bash` check, so a hook that names the table is refused. A daemon with a credential file configured is now also made non-dumpable, as #1157 already did for env credentials, because the table stays in its memory. Separately, the non-dumpable flag stops only processes without `CAP_SYS_PTRACE`. A daemon that holds a credential gets a loud startup warning naming the fix (run as non-root, or drop `SYS_PTRACE`) when it runs as root (real or effective uid 0) with that capability in its bounding or inheritable set, or holds it in its ambient set. It warns rather than refuses, so a privileged deployment that upgrades does not go down. `extras/scion/Dockerfile` still ends as root on purpose, since Scion picks the user with `--user`. Docker's default capabilities already drop `SYS_PTRACE`; the Scion page now says what changes in a privileged run. Design: `docs/credential-files-design.md`. ([#1201](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1201))
- **The multi-session bearer table can store token digests, so an agent that reads it learns nothing that authenticates.** A `users.json` row may now set `token_sha256`, the hex SHA-256 of its bearer token, instead of the plaintext `token`. The daemon hashes each presented token and compares digests in constant time. Before this, an agent whose `bash` reached a readable table by a spelling the #1267 gate can't see (a glob, a variable, `cd` plus a bare name) learned every token in it, and could inject a forged task as any identity, including a `task_from` dispatcher. New `core-agent auth hash-token` reads a token from stdin, never argv, and prints the row's value. `dev/tools/gen-users-json` now writes hashed tables. **Plaintext rows keep working unchanged**, and startup warns, naming each identity still stored that way (never its token). Daemons older than this release reject a `token_sha256` row as an unknown field, so upgrade every daemon that reads a table before hashing it. See `docs/hashed-bearer-tokens-design.md`. ([#1213](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1213), [#1269](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/pull/1269))
- **The local TUI no longer reads a local file because a relayed message named it.** core-tui v0.30.1 (go-steer/core-tui#364) expands `@`-references on an auto-continue turn only in the texts the operator queued in this TUI. A watcher's wake payload or an injected chat message containing `@/path` used to make the operator's TUI read that file into the model's prompt. ([go-steer/core-tui#364](https://github.com/go-steer/core-tui/issues/364))
- **Attach clients and the self-development rig no longer leave the attach token where the agent can read it.** `core-agent-tui`, `core-agent attach` and `core-agent ls` now *take* the token `--token-env` names: read it once, unset the variable so their own children — an `$EDITOR`, a clipboard helper — never inherit it, and on Linux make themselves non-dumpable, so a same-user process cannot read it back out of `/proc/<pid>/environ`, which unsetting does not change — from the moment the client reads it, that is: the few milliseconds between `exec` and that read stay open, and only a token that never travels in the environment (a `--token-file`, tracked in #1201) closes them. The agent runs as the operator's user in the self-development rig, and the token answers its permission prompts. The rig also stops writing the token to `RUN_DIR/attach.env` — `umask 077` kept out other users, not the agent — and prints the attach command with the token inline to its own terminal instead; its webhook sink takes its ingress token the same way, so the agent cannot forge a delivery the rig would accept. What remains is documented rather than claimed closed: the operator's shell history if it records space-prefixed commands, a tmux or screen scrollback the agent can capture as the same user, and anything else the same user can read. An agent unable to reach any approval credential takes a different user, not a different file layout. Follows #1157, which closed the daemon's side. ([#1201](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1201))
- **The agent can no longer reach the daemon's attach token, which answers its own permission prompts.** `--attach-token` read the token from an env var and left it there, and the `bash` tool inherited the daemon's whole environment — so test code the agent wrote, run by an approved `go test`, could approve a pending prompt, add a standing allow rule, or reset a tripped guardrail, with no direct gated call for an operator to notice. An alert target's `bearer_env` leaked the same way. Every env var the daemon resolves *by name* — each `*_env` config field, the same set `env.yaml`'s drift report reads, plus the name `--attach-token` gives, which may come from no config at all — is now withheld from every process the daemon starts: `bash` commands, hooks, MCP stdio servers and `sciontool`, via a new `pkg/childenv`. Startup lists what is withheld. **Scrubbing alone was measured not to work**: `/proc/<pid>/environ` is the environment the daemon was *started* with, untouched by anything it does to its own environment, and readable by any same-user process, so `cat /proc/$PPID/environ` from a scrubbed child returned the token verbatim. While it holds such a credential the daemon now also clears its dumpable flag on Linux (`prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)`), which closes that read, `/proc/<pid>/mem` and same-user `ptrace`; the cost is no core dumps and no attaching a debugger to a running daemon as the same user. The same names are never spliced into model-visible text: `${env:NAME}` for a withheld name interpolates to nothing, declared in `env.yaml` or not — skill resources are re-read on every load and loading one never prompts, so an agent could otherwise write `${env:…}` into a skill file and read the token straight back. **Behaviour change:** a hook that used one of these variables no longer sees it, and hooks have no `env` block to restore it; read the secret from a file instead. Fixing the MCP path surfaced a second leak: a stdio server whose `mcp.json` entry had no `env` block got a nil `exec.Cmd.Env` and so inherited everything; it is now always given the scrubbed environment, with an explicit `env` entry still applied on top. Provider and cloud credentials are deliberately not withheld, since SDKs rather than config consume them and a coding agent's own tooling needs them. Routes outside the daemon's process — an `export`ed token in the launching shell, attach clients holding it, a token-less loopback listener, credential files, a root daemon, and the self-dev rig writing the token to a file — are documented and tracked in [#1201](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1201). ([#1157](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1157))
- **The OpenTelemetry modules move to v1.45 and the ADK to v1.7.0, which clears two advisories `govulncheck` reported as reachable.** [GO-2026-6505](https://pkg.go.dev/vuln/GO-2026-6505) affects the OTLP trace exporter. [GO-2026-6615](https://pkg.go.dev/vuln/GO-2026-6615) is a busy-spin in the log SDK's `BatchProcessor` when its export buffer is full. Fixing the second needs `otel/log` v0.21, and ADK v1.7.0 is the first ADK release that builds against it, so the ADK moves from v1.2.0 and genai to v1.57. Changes you can see:
  - The ADK's `model.LLMResponse`, embedded in `session.Event`, gains `InputTranscription`, `OutputTranscription` and `SessionResumptionHandle`. They appear as null or empty keys wherever the attach API serializes an event, for example `GET /sessions/{sid}/agents/{name}/events`. The change is additive, and the protocol version is unchanged.
  - An MCP tool that returns only an image, a resource or nothing now succeeds with a placeholder or empty output, where it used to fail with "no text content in tool response".
  - A skill whose `allowed-tools` is a plain comma-separated scalar now loads, where it used to be dropped. The skill-loading system instruction now names `load_skill`'s real `name` argument, so expect one prompt-cache miss after upgrading.
  - ADK's spans count thinking tokens in `gen_ai.usage.output_tokens`, and rename the reasoning attribute to `gen_ai.usage.reasoning.output_tokens`. HTTP server metrics gain an `http.route` label.
  - The direct Gemini API with every built-in tool off now gets the same empty-response detection as every other Gemini configuration. ADK stopped flagging a stream of empty chunks, which would otherwise have ended the turn silently.
- **On a multi-session daemon, `POST /perms/allow` now takes the session owner or an admin, and an added pattern stays in its own session.** Runtime allow and deny patterns used to go into the daemon's shared policy. A contributor on one session could add `*`, which auto-allows every non-bash tool call, and it applied to every other session on the daemon, including ones that contributor couldn't read. Runtime patterns now go to the session's own gate, whether they come from `/allow`, `/deny` or `POST /perms/allow|deny` (the local TUI's `/allow` and `/deny` still also save to `.agents/config.json`, as before). They last as long as the session's live gate, so idle eviction, resume and restart drop them. Configured patterns still apply everywhere; a session's deny can narrow a configured allow, and a session's allow can't lift a configured deny. `/perms/allow` is held to the same bar as `/perms/mode`. `/perms/deny` only narrows, so contributors keep it. An "allow always" answer to a prompt is still daemon-wide; see the next bullet for who can give one. ([#1176](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1176))
- **On a multi-session daemon, only a daemon admin's "allow always" reaches beyond the session.** An always grant goes into the policy every session shares and persists to `.agents/config.json`, so a contributor could steer the model into a call, answer "always", and have every other session auto-allow it, including sessions they couldn't read. `POST /perms/respond` now applies an `allow-always` from anyone but a daemon admin as `allow-session`, and the response reports it with `decision` and `downgraded` (attach protocol 1.17.0). That includes the session's owner, because any authenticated caller can own a session by creating one. A daemon without `--multi-session` is unchanged. Separately, and whoever answers, an always grant now matches only the call that was approved: a `*`, `?`, `[` or `\` in the call is escaped in the saved pattern instead of acting as a wildcard, and an unbalanced `[` no longer makes the answer fail. Grants saved before this are read as written, so review `permissions.allow` for wildcard entries an "always" added. A hand-written allow entry containing `\*`, `\?`, `\[` or `\\` is now matched only as a glob, not also as the literal string. A daemon admin is an identity in `attach.multi_session.admin_identities`; with none configured, every HTTP "allow always" is downgraded. ([#1179](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/1179))

_Pre-release history: cut incrementally as `v3.0.0-dev.5` (2026-10-21), `v3.0.0-dev.4` (2026-10-20), `v3.0.0-dev.3` (2026-10-20), `v3.0.0-dev.2` (2026-10-20), `v3.0.0-dev.1` (2026-10-12), `v2.10.0-dev.1` (2026-09-17). The GitHub Release pages for each pre-release tag retain their historical release notes; this CHANGELOG folds them into the GA entry per Keep a Changelog convention._
